<html>
<head>
<title>Federal Register, Volume 86 Issue 134 (Friday, July 16, 2021)</title>
</head>
<body><pre>
[Federal Register Volume 86, Number 134 (Friday, July 16, 2021)]
[Rules and Regulations]
[Pages 37679-37681]
From the Federal Register Online via the Government Publishing Office [<a href="http://www.gpo.gov">www.gpo.gov</a>]
[FR Doc No: 2021-15162]


=======================================================================
-----------------------------------------------------------------------

DEPARTMENT OF EDUCATION

34 CFR Chapter II


Final Waiver and Extension of the Project Periods for the Equity 
Assistance Centers Grant Program

AGENCY: Office of Elementary and Secondary Education (OESE), Department 
of Education.

ACTION: Final waiver and extension of project periods.

-----------------------------------------------------------------------

SUMMARY: The Secretary waives the requirements in the Education 
Department General Administrative Regulations that generally prohibit 
project periods exceeding five years and project period extensions 
involving the obligation of additional Federal funds. The waiver and 
extension enable four projects under Assistance Listing Number (ALN) 
84.004D to receive funding for an additional period, not to exceed 
September 30, 2022.

DATES: The waiver and extension of the project periods are effective 
July 16, 2021.

FOR FURTHER INFORMATION CONTACT: Ed Vitelli, U.S. Department of 
Education, 400 Maryland Avenue SW, Room 3E106, Washington, DC 20202. 
Telephone: 202-453-6203. Email: <a href="/cdn-cgi/l/email-protection#115475667063753f477865747d7d785174753f767e67"><span class="__cf_email__" data-cfemail="d792b3a0b6a5b3f981bea3b2bbbbbe97b2b3f9b0b8a1">[email&#160;protected]</span></a>.
    If you use a telecommunications device for the deaf (TDD) or a text 
telephone (TTY), call the Federal Relay Service (FRS), toll free, at 1-
800-877-8339.

SUPPLEMENTARY INFORMATION:

Background

    On July 18, 2016, the Department of Education (Department) 
published in the Federal Register (81 FR 46820) a notice inviting 
applications for four projects for fiscal year (FY) 2016 under the 
Equity Assistance Centers (EAC) program, authorized under title IV of 
the Civil Rights Act of 1964, 42 U.S.C. 2000c-2000c-2, 2000c-5.
    The purpose of the EAC projects is to provide technical assistance 
(including training) at the request of school boards and other 
responsible governmental agencies in the preparation, adoption, and 
implementation of plans for the desegregation of public schools, and in 
the development of effective methods of coping with special educational 
problems occasioned by desegregation. Desegregation assistance, per 34 
CFR 270.4, may include, among other activities: (1) Dissemination of 
information regarding effective methods of coping with special 
educational problems occasioned by desegregation; (2) assistance and 
advice in coping with

[[Page 37680]]

these problems; and (3) training designed to improve the ability of 
teachers, supervisors, counselors, parents, community members, 
community organizations, and other elementary or secondary school 
personnel to deal effectively with special educational problems 
occasioned by desegregation. All four EAC projects provide technical 
assistance (including training) in all four of the desegregation 
assistance areas: Race, sex, national origin, and religion. A table 
listing the FY 2016 EAC projects follows.

------------------------------------------------------------------------
 FY 2016 awards under ALN 84.004D            Project information
------------------------------------------------------------------------
S004D160012.......................  Mid-Atlantic Equity Consortium,
                                     Bethesda, MD, Project: Center for
                                     Education Equity.
S004D160005.......................  Intercultural Development Research
                                     Association, San Antonio, TX,
                                     Project: IDRA Equity Assistance
                                     Center South.
S004D160011.......................  Indiana University, Indianapolis,
                                     IN, Project: Midwest and Plains
                                     Equity Assistance Center.
S004D160004.......................  Metropolitan State University of
                                     Denver, Denver, CO, Project:
                                     Western Educational Equity
                                     Assistance Center (WEEAC).
------------------------------------------------------------------------

    The EACs' project periods started on October 1, 2016 and will end 
on September 30, 2021.
    On January 13, 2021, the Department published in the Federal 
Register (86 FR 2653) a notice inviting applications for an FY 2021 EAC 
competition (FY 2021 NIA).
    However, as the effects of the COVID-19 pandemic unfolded and as 
learning recovery and school reentry efforts intensified, the 
Department sought to ensure the continuity of services provided by the 
FY 2016 EAC projects to vulnerable populations, schools, and school 
districts across the country. The Department also sought to ensure that 
the next EAC grant competition is, to the extent statutorily permitted, 
aligned with the Biden Administration's policy directives, including, 
for example, the Executive orders and memorandum included in the table 
below.

------------------------------------------------------------------------
       Title of policy directive         Date signed by President Biden
------------------------------------------------------------------------
Executive Order 13985: Advancing        January 20, 2021.
 Racial Equity and Support for
 Underserved Communities Through the
 Federal Government.
Executive Order 13988: Preventing and   January 20, 2021.
 Combating Discrimination on the Basis
 of Gender Identity or Sexual
 Orientation.
Memorandum: Condemning and Combating    January 26, 2021.
 Racism, Xenophobia, and Intolerance
 Against Asian Americans and Pacific
 Islanders in the United States.
Executive Order 14012: Restoring Faith  February 2, 2021.
 in Our Legal Immigration Systems and
 Strengthening Integration and
 Inclusion Efforts for New Americans.
------------------------------------------------------------------------

    To ensure the continuity of services, and to allow the Department 
the opportunity to consider how best to align the EAC program with 
these Executive orders, to the extent statutorily permitted, the 
Department published on March 4, 2021 in the Federal Register (86 FR 
12664) a notice withdrawing the FY 2021 NIA and cancelling the FY 2021 
EAC competition. On March 25, 2021, the Department published in the 
Federal Register (86 FR 15829) a notice of proposed waiver and 
extension, in which it proposed to waive the requirements in the 
Education Department General Administrative Regulations that generally 
prohibit project periods exceeding five years and project period 
extensions involving the obligation of additional Federal funds. The 
proposed waiver and extension would enable the four FY 2016 EAC 
projects to receive funding for one additional period, not to exceed 
September 30, 2022.
    Public Comment: In response to our invitation of public comment in 
the notice of proposed waiver and extension, one party submitted a 
responsive comment. An analysis of the comment follows.
    Comment: One commenter stated that three of the four FY 2016 EAC 
projects should not receive a waiver and extension because they have 
not demonstrated transparency and results related to their work in the 
EAC program. With respect to one of these projects, the commenter cited 
transactions that it contended constituted conflicts of interest or 
created the appearance of fiscal impropriety.
    Discussion: The Department encourages the commenter to visit the 
Department's EAC performance web page, located at <a href="https://oese.ed.gov/offices/office-of-formula-grants/program-and-grantee-support-services/training-and-advisory-services-equity-assistance-centers/performance-training-and-advisory-services-equity-assistance-centers/">https://oese.ed.gov/offices/office-of-formula-grants/program-and-grantee-support-services/training-and-advisory-services-equity-assistance-centers/performance-training-and-advisory-services-equity-assistance-centers/</a>, to find 
current performance data for the four FY 2016 EAC projects. In 
accordance with the applicable requirements for continuation funding in 
34 CFR 75.253, the Department monitored and reviewed the performance of 
the FY 2016 EAC projects, which, included, where appropriate, examining 
allegations of potential conflicts of interest and fiscal impropriety, 
and determined that all FY 2016 EAC projects demonstrated that they 
made substantial progress in achieving the goals and objectives of 
their grants, and in meeting their performance measures and targets 
within the scope of their approved projects. The Department also notes 
that all FY 2016 EAC projects have made their most recent single audits 
publicly available, in accordance with Federal grant regulations. 
Accordingly, the Department has concluded that continuation of the 
projects is in the best interest of the Federal Government.
    Changes: None.

Final Waivers and Extensions

    The Department believes it is in the best interest of the public to 
extend the current EAC project periods for one year. Correspondingly, 
the Secretary waives the requirements in 34 CFR 75.250, which prohibit 
project periods exceeding five years, as well as the requirements in 34 
CFR 75.261(a) and (c)(2), which permit the extension of a project 
period only if the extension does not involve the obligation of 
additional Federal funds. The waiver will permit the Department to 
issue a FY 2021 continuation award to each of the four

[[Page 37681]]

currently funded EAC projects, as estimated in the table below.

------------------------------------------------------------------------
                                                             Estimated
                   Project information                        amount
------------------------------------------------------------------------
Mid-Atlantic Equity Consortium, Project: Center for           $1,640,562
 Education Equity.......................................
Intercultural Development Research Association, Project:       1,641,567
 IDRA Equity Assistance Center South....................
Indiana University, Project: Midwest and Plains Equity         1,607,310
 Assistance Center......................................
Metropolitan State University of Denver, Project:              1,642,170
 Western Educational Equity Assistance Center (WEEAC)...
------------------------------------------------------------------------

    Please note that the Department is not bound by the estimates in 
this table.
    Any activities carried out under these continuation awards must be 
consistent with the scope and objectives of the grantees' applications 
as approved in the FY 2016 competition. The requirements for 
continuation awards are set forth in 34 CFR 75.253.

Regulatory Flexibility Act Certification

    The Secretary certifies that the waiver and extension of the 
project period would not have a significant economic impact on a 
substantial number of small entities. The only entities that would be 
affected by the waiver and extension of the project period are the 
current grantees. The Secretary certifies that the waiver and extension 
would not have a significant economic impact on these entities, because 
the extension of an existing project period imposes minimal compliance 
costs, and the activities required to support the additional year of 
funding would not impose additional regulatory burdens or require 
unnecessary Federal supervision.

Paperwork Reduction Act of 1995

    This notice of final waiver and extension of the project period 
does not contain any information collection requirements.

Intergovernmental Review

    This program is subject to Executive Order 12372 and the 
regulations in 34 CFR part 79. One of the objectives of the Executive 
order is to foster an intergovernmental partnership and a strengthened 
federalism. The Executive order relies on processes developed by State 
and local governments for coordination and review of proposed Federal 
financial assistance. This document provides notification of our 
specific plans and actions for this program.
    Accessible Format: On request to the program contact person listed 
under FOR FURTHER INFORMATION CONTACT, individuals with disabilities 
can obtain this document in an accessible format. The Department will 
provide the requestor with an accessible format that may include Rich 
Text Format (RTF) or text format (txt), a thumb drive, an MP3 file, 
braille, large print, audiotape, or compact disc, or other accessible 
format.
    Electronic Access to This Document: The official version of this 
document is the document published in the Federal Register. You may 
access the official edition of the Federal Register and the Code of 
Federal Regulations at <a href="http://www.govinfo.gov">www.govinfo.gov</a>. At this site you can view this 
document, as well as all other documents of this Department published 
in the Federal Register, in text or Portable Document Format (PDF). To 
use PDF, you must have Adobe Acrobat Reader, which is available free at 
the site.
    You may also access documents of the Department published in the 
Federal Register by using the article search feature at 
<a href="http://www.federalregister.gov">www.federalregister.gov</a>. Specifically, through the advanced search 
feature at this site, you can limit your search to documents published 
by the Department.

Ian Rosenblum,
Deputy Assistant Secretary for Policy and Programs Delegated the 
Authority to Perform the Functions and Duties of the Assistant 
Secretary, Office of Elementary and Secondary Education.
[FR Doc. 2021-15162 Filed 7-15-21; 8:45 am]

Its only commands are creation of the board, a thirty-day report deadline and a recited statutory standstill. Under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 (report deadline, option C) none of those ends the order; the first held end is the express revocation by EO 12553 on 1986-02-25 (signature date, no effective-date wording). The board's report date 1954-11-20 is a spent fact recorded in the clause reason. Savings: none found in EO 12553 or in the order itself. Searches: U.S. Code and eCFR number-citation searches found no hit for this order; the OFR disposition-note search lists only the EO 12553 revocation in the chain.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Checked for an earlier end: (1) the order's own terms contain a report deadline and a post-report standstill but no sunset, so the report and standstill dates are spent facts and not ends under the report-deadline ruling; (2) no extension, amendment or revocation of EO 10570 other than EO 12553 appears in the dossier chain or in the number-citation scan of held texts; the report-time extensions described in the held report (where a report is held) were stipulations approved by the President, not held instruments, and affect only the deadline; (3) a general later rule is not treated as an end, only EO 12553, which names the order by number.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The standstill is recited as provided by section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, so any no-change duty it describes arises under that statute and not only from the order; by the order's own terms it ran only to thirty days after the board's report, which was long before 1986. The order creates no continuing right, office or appropriation. No other command in the order survives the board's report.\",\"savings_check\":\"The text of EO 12553 contains no savings, exception, effective-date or notwithstanding wording (the held text was searched for those terms). The board's investigation, report and the standstill were spent in the 1950s, and the order created no accrued right or later-effective duty. The statute the order invokes is not affected by revoking the order.\",\"scope_check\":\"One clause covers the whole order; the order has no partial-scope command, no exception and no delayed effective date. The revocation in EO 12553 has no exception text, so it reaches the whole order.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"e9c80d0c381d5045f956cd6a0abb24f9683079df4265dbb4c01a66e1e97cdc1a","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g05/packet/complete-v1/receipts/research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g05/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:9","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"},{"file_sha256":"e9c80d0c381d5045f956cd6a0abb24f9683079df4265dbb4c01a66e1e97cdc1a","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g05/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:9","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:10570#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":["state_at_cutoff","first_end","later_events"],"scope_id":"EO:10570#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","state":"match"},"sha256":"945348afbba863ae7dc1fbe7df8759682fe1733962734a9255a1062c0561048c","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","text_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"c2526a4a8262082976cb5e2140daba3cdc4716246a48e720f42430099e88902b","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10570.recheck.txt","sha256":"c2526a4a8262082976cb5e2140daba3cdc4716246a48e720f42430099e88902b","state":"match"},"sha256":"a8edb23032c98ee2a1c98f1851ce996b701eda1109ec256e3d56e3c3e1ef9d06","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10570.recheck.txt","text_sha256":"c2526a4a8262082976cb5e2140daba3cdc4716246a48e720f42430099e88902b"}]}]}

The held text says the earlier order \"is hereby revoked.\" The act has no deferred effective date, condition, report or continuing duty, so it was performed and exhausted when signed on February 8, 1940. No instrument held in the repository revokes, amends or extends Executive Order 8341, so no earlier or later end displaces that date. The working text is the official Federal Register page text (machine-extracted from the PDF, OCR), not an authenticated transcription; the page number and date were read from it.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier end exists: the order is itself a revocation executed on signing, with no stated later effective date, so nothing precedes February 8, 1940. The date is the signing date, which the held text gives as \\\"February 8, 1940.\\\" Under 44 U.S.C. 1507 a document required to be published is not valid against a person without actual knowledge until filed; the held text shows filing on February 9, 1940, and the issue date is February 10, 1940. The alternative readings (filing or issue date) are disclosed; none changes the conclusion that the revocation was performed in the first days of February 1940. The packet's later-event chain is empty, the machine searches (U.S. Code, eCFR as of 2026-10-14, Office of the Federal Register disposition notes covering 1937 onward) return zero hits for this order, and the bounded held-text search found no later order citing it by number.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order contains no independent command, delegation, reporting duty or continuing instruction. The only operative content is the revocation of Executive Order No. 8323, and that content is exhausted when the revocation took effect.\",\"savings_check\":\"The order has no savings clause. Its consequence, the revocation of Executive Order No. 8323, is a surviving effect of an executed act, not an unperformed command of Executive Order 8341, and no held source shows any separate duty, right or pending action left by it.\",\"scope_check\":\"The packet inventory has one clause (the sole paragraph, characters 1046 to 1635 of the registered text), which I confirmed against the whole held order: heading, the single operative paragraph, signature, date and Federal Register filing line. There is no second section, so the inventory is complete. The one clause is a one-time revocation, terminated by performance on February 8, 1940.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:8341#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":["state_at_cutoff","first_end","later_events"],"scope_id":"EO:8341#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"1d4c69cd6e853fa6f75cf77a91c8d7beb86b685df5daadb091d616f9fa0a6681","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_8341.txt","sha256":"1d4c69cd6e853fa6f75cf77a91c8d7beb86b685df5daadb091d616f9fa0a6681","state":"match"},"sha256":"7f27ace7dd1e7d0878cc9fedf7b7984f89e101ceaf1db38ac106f6d598061159","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_8341.txt","text_sha256":"1d4c69cd6e853fa6f75cf77a91c8d7beb86b685df5daadb091d616f9fa0a6681"}]}]}

Both clauses were spent by their own terms: clause 2 was performed when the order was published in the Federal Register issue dated December 18, 1940 (the filing line reads December 17, 1940), and clause 1 applied to the single calendar day December 24, 1940 and had no further subject after that day. The end date for clause 1 is recorded as December 24, 1940 inclusive (the order runs through that day); the exclusive reading, under which it ends when December 24 begins, is the alternative a reviewer should see. No instrument held in the repository revokes, extends or amends the order. The working text is the official Federal Register page text (machine-extracted from the PDF, OCR), not an authenticated transcription.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier end for clause 1: it is a one-day closure and excusal, with no revocation, extension or amendment in the held record (no later-event chain events; zero hits in the U.S. Code, eCFR and Office of the Federal Register disposition-note searches; the bounded held-text search found no later order citing it by number). A report-style deadline is not at issue; the day named is the order's own period. Clause 2 ended earlier, on performance: the held Federal Register page for 5 FR 5143 is the issue headed \\\"Washington, Wednesday, December 18, 1940\\\" and carries the order, which shows the direction to publish was carried out by December 18, 1940. The whole order therefore ended on the later of the two, December 24, 1940.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"No independent command exists. The exceptions in paragraph 1 qualify the closure and excusal for December 24, 1940 only; they confer no standing power. Paragraph 2 is a one-time publication direction, performed on publication.\",\"savings_check\":\"The order has no savings clause. Employees' excused absence on December 24, 1940 is an accrued past consequence of the executed closure, not a continuing command; no held source shows any pending right or duty beyond that day.\",\"scope_check\":\"Clause inventory confirmed against the whole held order: heading, the introductory authority recital, paragraph 1 (the closure and the excusal of employees, with its exceptions for employees excluded by agency heads or whose absence would be inconsistent with existing law) and paragraph 2 (publication), then the signature, date and filing line. The packet's two clauses correspond to paragraphs 1 and 2 exactly. The excusal and the exceptions are one sentence of paragraph 1 and share its end.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:8613#1"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":["state_at_cutoff","first_end","later_events"],"scope_id":"EO:8613#1"},{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:8613#2"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":["state_at_cutoff","first_end","later_events"],"scope_id":"EO:8613#2"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"8a9986775fe5f16dee8fcff997de078ce2b4385af45371dc465c2a22b3c1a281","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_8613.txt","sha256":"8a9986775fe5f16dee8fcff997de078ce2b4385af45371dc465c2a22b3c1a281","state":"match"},"sha256":"3623e63295a54269b1f45cad629234bb959b209e81b91c69440edf0b64fc73b9","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_8613.txt","text_sha256":"8a9986775fe5f16dee8fcff997de078ce2b4385af45371dc465c2a22b3c1a281"}]}]}

it was performed and the command exhausted at the close of business on January 15, 1941. The loss of port-of-entry status is a surviving consequence of that executed act, not a continuing command. No instrument held in the repository revokes, amends or restores the designation by executive order. The working text is the official Federal Register page text (machine-extracted from the PDF, OCR), not an authenticated transcription.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"No earlier end exists: the order is the revocation itself and states its own effective moment, the close of business January 15, 1941, so nothing can precede that moment. The packet's later-event chain is empty, the three machine searches (U.S. Code, eCFR as of 2026-10-14, Office of the Federal Register disposition notes covering 1937 onward) return zero hits, and the bounded held-text search found no later order citing it by number. A later restoration of the port designation by an executive order would be a separate instrument that would not revive this one; none is held.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"There is no independent command, delegation or continuing instruction. The only operative content is the revocation of the designation of Unalaska, and the order's own effective-date sentence fixes when it takes effect.\",\"savings_check\":\"The order has no savings clause. The consequence, that Unalaska is no longer a designated customs port of entry in District No. 31, is a surviving effect of an executed act, not a continuing command of the order, and no held source shows any pending duty under it.\",\"scope_check\":\"The packet inventory has one clause, the operative paragraph with the effective-date sentence (characters 2351 to 2733 of the registered text). I confirmed it against the whole held order: heading, the single operative sentence invoking section 1 of the Act of August 1, 1914 (38 Stat. 609, 623; 19 U.S.C. 2), the effective-date sentence, signature, date and filing line. The effective-date sentence is part of the same clause and fixes its end. No other clause exists.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:8639#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":["state_at_cutoff","first_end","later_events"],"scope_id":"EO:8639#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"9c38edfdf1474b1699a4f7d384be1b5e94bc927706822d388451b946e25897fe","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_8639.txt","sha256":"9c38edfdf1474b1699a4f7d384be1b5e94bc927706822d388451b946e25897fe","state":"match"},"sha256":"055484d7877eb8e43853025cd41e1f7078fc28946ddc573aad4a51572fba51ca","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_8639.txt","text_sha256":"9c38edfdf1474b1699a4f7d384be1b5e94bc927706822d388451b946e25897fe"}]}]}

Preparation attestations never approve legal conclusions or replace whole-case review.
